import { $internal } from '../common';
import type { Entity } from '../entity/types';
import { getEntityId } from '../entity/utils/pack-entity';
import { setRelationData } from '../relation/relation';
import type { Relation, RelationPair, RelationTarget } from '../relation/types';
import { isRelationPair } from '../relation/utils/is-relation';
import { Store } from '../storage';
import { getStore } from '../trait/trait';
import type { Trait } from '../trait/types';
import { shallowEqual } from '../utils/shallow-equal';
import type { World } from '../world';
import { isModifier } from './modifier';
import { setChanged, setPairChanged } from './modifiers/changed';
import { getPairTrackedData } from './utils/pair-tracking';
import type {
    InstancesFromParameters,
    QueryInstance,
    QueryParameter,
    QueryResult,
    QueryResultOptions,
    StoresFromParameters,
} from './types';

type PairResolver = {
    relation: Relation<Trait>;
    target: Entity;
};

type ChangedPair = [Entity, Trait, Entity?];

export function createQueryResult<T extends QueryParameter[]>(
    world: World,
    entities: Entity[],
    query: QueryInstance,
    params: QueryParameter[]
): QueryResult<T> {
    const traits: Trait[] = [];
    const stores: Store<any>[] = [];
    const pairResolvers: (PairResolver | undefined)[] = [];

    getQueryStores(params, traits, stores, pairResolvers, world);

    const results = Object.assign(entities, {
        readEach(
            callback: (state: InstancesFromParameters<T>, entity: Entity, index: number) => void
        ) {
            const state = Array.from({ length: traits.length }) as InstancesFromParameters<T>;

            for (let i = 0; i < entities.length; i++) {
                const entity = entities[i];

                // Create snapshots without atomic tracking
                createSnapshots(world, entity, traits, stores, pairResolvers, state);

                callback(state, entity, i);
            }

            return results;
        },

        updateEach(
            callback: (state: InstancesFromParameters<T>, entity: Entity, index: number) => void,
            options: QueryResultOptions = { changeDetection: 'auto' }
        ) {
            const state = Array.from({ length: traits.length });

            // Inline all three permutations of updateEach for performance.
            if (options.changeDetection === 'auto') {
                const changedPairs: ChangedPair[] = [];
                const atomicSnapshots: any[] = [];
                const trackedIndices: number[] = [];
                const untrackedIndices: number[] = [];

                getTrackedTraits(traits, world, query, trackedIndices, untrackedIndices);

                for (let i = 0; i < entities.length; i++) {
                    const entity = entities[i];
                    const eid = getEntityId(entity);

                    createSnapshotsWithAtomic(
                        world,
                        entity,
                        traits,
                        stores,
                        pairResolvers,
                        state,
                        atomicSnapshots
                    );
                    callback(state as unknown as InstancesFromParameters<T>, entity, i);

                    // Skip if the entity has been destroyed.
                    if (!world.has(entity)) continue;

                    // Commit all changes back to the stores for tracked traits.
                    for (let j = 0; j < trackedIndices.length; j++) {
                        const index = trackedIndices[j];
                        const trait = traits[index];
                        const resolver = pairResolvers[index];
                        const ctx = trait[$internal];
                        const newValue = state[index];
                        const store = stores[index];

                        let changed = false;
                        if (resolver) {
                            changed = !shallowEqual(newValue, atomicSnapshots[index]);
                            if (changed) {
                                setRelationData(world, entity, resolver.relation, resolver.target, newValue);
                            }
                        } else if (ctx.type === 'aos') {
                            changed = ctx.fastSetWithChangeDetection(eid, store, newValue);
                            if (!changed) {
                                changed = !shallowEqual(newValue, atomicSnapshots[index]);
                            }
                        } else {
                            changed = ctx.fastSetWithChangeDetection(eid, store, newValue);
                        }

                        // Collect changed traits.
                        if (changed) {
                            changedPairs.push(
                                resolver ? [entity, trait, resolver.target] : [entity, trait]
                            );
                        }
                    }

                    // Commit all changes back to the stores for untracked traits.
                    for (let j = 0; j < untrackedIndices.length; j++) {
                        const index = untrackedIndices[j];
                        const trait = traits[index];
                        const resolver = pairResolvers[index];
                        const ctx = trait[$internal];
                        const store = stores[index];

                        if (resolver) {
                            setRelationData(world, entity, resolver.relation, resolver.target, state[index]);
                        } else {
                            ctx.fastSet(eid, store, state[index]);
                        }
                    }
                }

                // Trigger change events for each entity that was modified.
                for (let i = 0; i < changedPairs.length; i++) {
                    const [entity, trait, target] = changedPairs[i];
                    if (target !== undefined) setPairChanged(world, entity, trait, target);
                    else setChanged(world, entity, trait);
                }
            } else if (options.changeDetection === 'always') {
                const changedPairs: ChangedPair[] = [];
                const atomicSnapshots: any[] = [];

                for (let i = 0; i < entities.length; i++) {
                    const entity = entities[i];
                    const eid = getEntityId(entity);

                    createSnapshotsWithAtomic(
                        world,
                        entity,
                        traits,
                        stores,
                        pairResolvers,
                        state,
                        atomicSnapshots
                    );
                    callback(state as unknown as InstancesFromParameters<T>, entity, i);

                    // Skip if the entity has been destroyed.
                    if (!world.has(entity)) continue;

                    // Commit all changes back to the stores.
                    for (let j = 0; j < traits.length; j++) {
                        const trait = traits[j];
                        const resolver = pairResolvers[j];
                        const ctx = trait[$internal];
                        const newValue = state[j];

                        let changed = false;
                        if (resolver) {
                            changed = !shallowEqual(newValue, atomicSnapshots[j]);
                            if (changed) {
                                setRelationData(world, entity, resolver.relation, resolver.target, newValue);
                            }
                        } else if (ctx.type === 'aos') {
                            changed = ctx.fastSetWithChangeDetection(eid, stores[j], newValue);
                            if (!changed) {
                                changed = !shallowEqual(newValue, atomicSnapshots[j]);
                            }
                        } else {
                            changed = ctx.fastSetWithChangeDetection(eid, stores[j], newValue);
                        }

                        // Collect changed traits.
                        if (changed) {
                            changedPairs.push(
                                resolver ? [entity, trait, resolver.target] : [entity, trait]
                            );
                        }
                    }
                }

                // Trigger change events for each entity that was modified.
                for (let i = 0; i < changedPairs.length; i++) {
                    const [entity, trait, target] = changedPairs[i];
                    if (target !== undefined) setPairChanged(world, entity, trait, target);
                    else setChanged(world, entity, trait);
                }
            } else if (options.changeDetection === 'never') {
                for (let i = 0; i < entities.length; i++) {
                    const entity = entities[i];
                    const eid = getEntityId(entity);
                    createSnapshots(world, entity, traits, stores, pairResolvers, state);
                    callback(state as unknown as InstancesFromParameters<T>, entity, i);

                    // Skip if the entity has been destroyed.
                    if (!world.has(entity)) continue;

                    // Commit all changes back to the stores.
                    for (let j = 0; j < traits.length; j++) {
                        const trait = traits[j];
                        const resolver = pairResolvers[j];
                        const ctx = trait[$internal];
                        if (resolver) {
                            setRelationData(world, entity, resolver.relation, resolver.target, state[j]);
                        } else {
                            ctx.fastSet(eid, stores[j], state[j]);
                        }
                    }
                }
            }

            return results;
        },

        useStores(callback: (stores: StoresFromParameters<T>, entities: readonly Entity[]) => void) {
            callback(stores as unknown as StoresFromParameters<T>, entities);
            return results;
        },

        select<U extends QueryParameter[]>(...params: U): QueryResult<U> {
            traits.length = 0;
            stores.length = 0;
            pairResolvers.length = 0;
            getQueryStores(params, traits, stores, pairResolvers, world);
            return results as unknown as QueryResult<U>;
        },

        sort(
            callback: (a: Entity, b: Entity) => number = (a, b) => getEntityId(a) - getEntityId(b)
        ): QueryResult<T> {
            Array.prototype.sort.call(entities, callback);
            return results;
        },
    });

    return results;
}

/* @inline */ function getTrackedTraits(
    traits: Trait[],
    world: World,
    query: QueryInstance,
    trackedIndices: number[],
    untrackedIndices: number[]
) {
    for (let i = 0; i < traits.length; i++) {
        const trait = traits[i];
        const hasTracked = world[$internal].trackedTraits.has(trait);
        const hasChanged = query.hasChangedModifiers && query.changedTraits.has(trait);

        if (hasTracked || hasChanged) trackedIndices.push(i);
        else untrackedIndices.push(i);
    }
}

/* @inline */ function createSnapshots(
    world: World,
    entity: Entity,
    traits: Trait[],
    stores: Store<any>[],
    pairResolvers: (PairResolver | undefined)[],
    state: any[]
) {
    const entityId = getEntityId(entity);
    for (let i = 0; i < traits.length; i++) {
        const trait = traits[i];
        const resolver = pairResolvers[i];
        if (resolver) {
            state[i] = getPairTrackedData(world, entity, resolver.relation, resolver.target);
        } else {
            const ctx = trait[$internal];
            state[i] = ctx.get(entityId, stores[i]);
        }
    }
}

/* @inline */ function createSnapshotsWithAtomic(
    world: World,
    entity: Entity,
    traits: Trait[],
    stores: Store<any>[],
    pairResolvers: (PairResolver | undefined)[],
    state: any[],
    atomicSnapshots: any[]
) {
    const entityId = getEntityId(entity);
    for (let j = 0; j < traits.length; j++) {
        const trait = traits[j];
        const resolver = pairResolvers[j];
        let value: any;
        if (resolver) {
            value = getPairTrackedData(world, entity, resolver.relation, resolver.target);
        } else {
            const ctx = trait[$internal];
            value = ctx.get(entityId, stores[j]);
        }
        state[j] = value;
        atomicSnapshots[j] = value && typeof value === 'object' ? { ...value } : value;
    }
}

/* @inline */ export function getQueryStores<T extends QueryParameter[]>(
    params: T,
    traits: Trait[],
    stores: Store<any>[],
    pairResolvers: (PairResolver | undefined)[],
    world: World
) {
    for (let i = 0; i < params.length; i++) {
        const param = params[i];

        // Handle relation pairs
        if (isRelationPair(param)) {
            pushPairResolver(param, traits, stores, pairResolvers, world);
            continue;
        }

        if (isModifier(param)) {
            // Skip not modifier.
            if (param.type === 'not') continue;

            const pairs = param.pairs;
            const modifierTraits = param.traits;

            // Collect pair-backed trait ids so regular traits aren't duplicated.
            const pairTraitIds = new Set<number>();
            if (pairs) {
                for (const pair of pairs) {
                    const pairCtx = pair[$internal];
                    const baseTrait = pairCtx.relation[$internal].trait;
                    pairTraitIds.add(baseTrait.id);
                    pushPairResolver(pair, traits, stores, pairResolvers, world);
                }
            }

            for (const trait of modifierTraits) {
                if (pairTraitIds.has(trait.id)) continue; // already pushed via pair resolver
                if (trait[$internal].type === 'tag') continue; // Skip tags
                traits.push(trait);
                stores.push(getStore(world, trait));
                pairResolvers.push(undefined);
            }
        } else {
            const trait = param as Trait;
            if (trait[$internal].type === 'tag') continue; // Skip tags
            traits.push(trait);
            stores.push(getStore(world, trait));
            pairResolvers.push(undefined);
        }
    }
}

/* @inline */ function pushPairResolver(
    pair: RelationPair,
    traits: Trait[],
    stores: Store<any>[],
    pairResolvers: (PairResolver | undefined)[],
    world: World
) {
    const pairCtx = pair[$internal];
    const relation = pairCtx.relation as Relation<Trait>;
    const target = pairCtx.target as RelationTarget;
    const baseTrait = relation[$internal].trait;

    if (baseTrait[$internal].type === 'tag') return;

    traits.push(baseTrait);
    stores.push(getStore(world, baseTrait));
    pairResolvers.push(typeof target === 'number' ? { relation, target } : undefined);
}

export function createEmptyQueryResult(): QueryResult<QueryParameter[]> {
    const results = Object.assign([], {
        readEach: () => results,
        updateEach: () => results,
        useStores: () => results,
        select: () => results,
        sort: () => results,
    }) as QueryResult<QueryParameter[]>;

    return results;
}

// Cached no-op result methods for relation-only queries
const relationOnlyMethods = {
    readEach(this: QueryResult<any>, callback: any) {
        // No traits to read, just iterate entities
        for (let i = 0; i < this.length; i++) {
            callback([], this[i], i);
        }
        return this;
    },
    updateEach(this: QueryResult<any>, callback: any) {
        // No traits to update, just iterate entities
        for (let i = 0; i < this.length; i++) {
            callback([], this[i], i);
        }
        return this;
    },
    useStores(this: QueryResult<any>, callback: any) {
        // No stores, call with empty array
        callback([], this);
        return this;
    },
    select(this: QueryResult<any>) {
        // No-op, nothing to select
        return this;
    },
};

/**
 * Lightweight query result for relation-only queries.
 * Skips store/trait setup since we only need to iterate entities.
 */
export function createRelationOnlyQueryResult<T extends QueryParameter[]>(
    entities: Entity[]
): QueryResult<T> {
    const results = Object.assign(entities, {
        readEach: relationOnlyMethods.readEach,
        updateEach: relationOnlyMethods.updateEach,
        useStores: relationOnlyMethods.useStores,
        select: relationOnlyMethods.select,
        sort(
            callback: (a: Entity, b: Entity) => number = (a, b) => getEntityId(a) - getEntityId(b)
        ): QueryResult<T> {
            Array.prototype.sort.call(entities, callback);
            return results;
        },
    }) as unknown as QueryResult<T>;

    return results;
}

Now there's a potential issue: in `getQueryStores`, for a modifier with pairs, I push pair resolvers for each pair, but if the modifier also has a
pair with wildcard target, `pushPairResolver` pushes raw base trait (resolver undefined). Then in `pairTraitIds`, the wildcard pair's base trait id is
added, and regular loop skips it. That means a wildcard pair pushes one raw entry. If both `Likes('*')` and `Likes(alice)` in same modifier,
pairTraitIds has Likes; both pairs push (raw for wildcard, resolver for alice) → two entries. Fine.

But there's a subtle bug: for `Added(Likes(alice), Likes(bob))`, modifierTraits = [Likes, Likes], pairs both specific. pushPairResolver pushes two
resolver entries. Then regular loop skips both Likes. Good.

Now, `getQueryStores` is exported and may be used elsewhere with old signature (4 args). Let me check callers.

STEP 81 SOURCE agent
REASONING_CONTENT
Existing tests pass. Now let me write a reproduction script for the new functionality to verify my implementation works. Let me write a comprehensive
probe.

Test cases:
1. `Added(Likes(alice))` detects adding Likes(alice) when entity already has Likes(bob) (non-first pair).
2. `Removed(Likes(alice))` detects removing Likes(alice) while entity still has Likes(bob) (non-last pair).
3. Exclusive replacement produces both removal and addition: `Added(Targeting(bob))` and `Removed(Targeting(alice))`.
4. Wildcard: `Added(Likes('*'))` matches any pair.
5. Opposite events cancel: add Likes(alice) then remove Likes(alice) → neither Added nor Removed matches.
6. Entity destruction fires pair-level removal: `Removed(Likes(alice))` matches when entity destroyed.
7. Or composition: `Or(Added(Likes(alice)), Added(Fears(bob)))`.
8. Different pair targets distinct cached queries: `Added(Likes(alice))` vs `Added(Likes(bob))` are different query instances.
9. Pair modifier + regular trait in same query: `Added(Likes(alice)), Position` requires both.
10. entity.changed accepts RelationPair: `Changed(Likes(alice))`.
11. Query result iteration resolves per-target data: `Added(Contains(gold))` readEach gives `{ amount: 42 }`, and `Removed(Contains(gold))` gives 42.
12. Modifier factories long-lived across world reset.

Let me write the probe.
MESSAGE
Existing tests pass. Now let me write a comprehensive probe for the new pair-level tracking features:
CALL s81_c0 TOOL_ID call_00_KOsDZClIZ2PTDMlJg5jc2987
FUNCTION bash
ARGUMENTS
command:
cd /app/packages/core && cat > probe.ts <<'EOF'
import { createWorld, relation, createAdded, createRemoved, createChanged, Or, $internal } from './src';

let pass = 0, fail = 0;
function assert(cond: any, msg: string) {
  if (cond) { pass++; console.log('  ok:', msg); }
  else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL:', msg); }
}

const world = createWorld();
world.init();

// 1. Non-first pair addition
{
  const Likes = relation();
  const Added = createAdded();
  const alice = world.spawn();
  const bob = world.spawn();
  const person = world.spawn();
  person.add(Likes(bob));
  person.add(Likes(alice)); // non-first addition
  const q = world.query(Added(Likes(alice)));
  assert(q.includes(person), 'non-first pair addition detected at pair level');
  world.query(Added(Likes(alice))); // drain
  world.reset();
}

// 2. Non-last pair removal
{
  const Likes = relation();
  const Removed = createRemoved();
  const alice = world.spawn();
  const bob = world.spawn();
  const person = world.spawn(Likes(alice), Likes(bob));
  person.remove(Likes(alice)); // non-last removal
  const q = world.query(Removed(Likes(alice)));
  assert(q.includes(person), 'non-last pair removal detected at pair level');
  world.query(Removed(Likes(alice))); // drain
  world.reset();
}

// 3. Exclusive replacement produces removal + addition
{
  const Targeting = relation({ exclusive: true });
  const Added = createAdded();
  const Removed = createRemoved();
  const a = world.spawn();
  const b = world.spawn();
  const enemy = world.spawn(Targeting(a));
  world.query(Added(Targeting('*'))); // drain
  world.query(Removed(Targeting('*'))); // drain
  enemy.add(Targeting(b)); // replaces a
  const addQ = world.query(Added(Targeting(b)));
  const remQ = world.query(Removed(Targeting(a)));
  assert(addQ.includes(enemy), 'exclusive replacement fires addition for new pair');
  assert(remQ.includes(enemy), 'exclusive replacement fires removal for old pair');
  world.reset();
}

// 4. Wildcard
{
  const Likes = relation();
  const Added = createAdded();
  const apple = world.spawn();
  const person = world.spawn();
  person.add(Likes(apple));
  const q = world.query(Added(Likes('*')));
  assert(q.includes(person), "wildcard Added(Likes('*')) matches pair addition");
  world.query(Added(Likes('*'))); // drain
  world.reset();
}

// 5. Opposite pair events cancel on same target
{
  const Likes = relation();
  const Added = createAdded();
  const Removed = createRemoved();
  const alice = world.spawn();
  const person = world.spawn();
  person.add(Likes(alice));
  person.remove(Likes(alice));
  const addQ = world.query(Added(Likes(alice)));
  const remQ = world.query(Removed(Likes(alice)));
  assert(addQ.length === 0, 'add+remove cancels Added');
  assert(remQ.length === 0, 'add+remove cancels Removed');
  world.reset();
}

// 6. Entity destruction fires pair-level removal
{
  const Likes = relation();
  const Removed = createRemoved();
  const alice = world.spawn();
  const person = world.spawn(Likes(alice));
  person.destroy();
  const q = world.query(Removed(Likes(alice)));
  assert(q.includes(person), 'entity destruction fires pair-level removal');
  world.reset();
}

// 7. Or composition
{
  const Likes = relation();
  const Fears = relation();
  const Added = createAdded();
  const apple = world.spawn();
  const ghost = world.spawn();
  const person = world.spawn();
  person.add(Likes(apple));
  const q = world.query(Or(Added(Likes(apple)), Added(Fears(ghost))));
  assert(q.includes(person), 'Or(Added(Likes(apple)), Added(Fears(ghost))) matches');
  world.query(Or(Added(Likes(apple)), Added(Fears(ghost)))); // drain
  world.reset();
}

// 8. Distinct cached queries per target
{
  const Likes = relation();
  const Added = createAdded();
  const alice = world.spawn();
  const bob = world.spawn();
  const ctx = world[$internal];
  const qa = world.query(Added(Likes(alice)));
  const qb = world.query(Added(Likes(bob)));
  assert(qa !== qb, 'different pair targets produce distinct cached queries');
  // entities returned separately
  const p1 = world.spawn(Likes(alice));
  const p2 = world.spawn(Likes(bob));
  world.query(Added(Likes(alice))); // drain
  world.query(Added(Likes(bob))); // drain
  world.reset();
}

// 9. Pair modifier + regular trait combined
{
  const Likes = relation();
  const Position = { id: 999 } as any; // will use real trait below
  world.reset();
}
{
  const Likes = relation();
  const Position = (await import('./src')).trait();
  const Added = createAdded();
  const alice = world.spawn();
  const person = world.spawn(Position);
  person.add(Likes(alice));
  const q = world.query(Added(Likes(alice)), Position);
  assert(q.includes(person), 'pair modifier + regular trait both satisfied');
  world.query(Added(Likes(alice)), Position); // drain

  const person2 = world.spawn();
  person2.add(Likes(alice));
  const q2 = world.query(Added(Likes(alice)), Position);
  assert(!q2.includes(person2), 'pair modifier + regular trait requires regular trait');
  world.reset();
}

// 10. entity.changed accepts RelationPair
{
  const Likes = relation({ store: { amount: 0 } });
  const Changed = createChanged();
  const alice = world.spawn();
  const person = world.spawn(Likes(alice, { amount: 1 }));
  world.query(Changed(Likes(alice))); // drain
  person.changed(Likes(alice));
  const q = world.query(Changed(Likes(alice)));
  assert(q.includes(person), 'entity.changed(RelationPair) triggers pair change');
  world.reset();
}

// 11. Query result iteration resolves per-target data
{
  const Contains = relation({ store: { amount: 0 } });
  const Added = createAdded();
  const gold = world.spawn();
  const inventory = world.spawn();
  inventory.add(Contains(gold, { amount: 42 }));
  let data: any;
  world.query(Added(Contains(gold))).readEach(([d]: any) => { data = d; });
  assert(data && data.amount === 42, 'Added pair query iteration exposes per-target data');
  world.query(Added(Contains(gold))); // drain
  world.reset();
}
{
  const Contains = relation({ store: { amount: 0 } });
  const Removed = createRemoved();
  const gold = world.spawn();
  const inventory = world.spawn();
  inventory.add(Contains(gold, { amount: 42 }));
  inventory.remove(Contains(gold));
  let data: any;
  const q = world.query(Removed(Contains(gold)));
  assert(q.length === 1, 'Removed pair query returns entity');
  q.readEach(([d]: any) => { data = d; });
  assert(data && data.amount === 42, 'Removed pair query iteration exposes removed pair data');
  world.reset();
}

// 12. Long-lived factories across world reset
{
  const Likes = relation();
  const Added = createAdded();
  const alice = world.spawn();
  world.reset();
  const person = world.spawn();
  person.add(Likes(alice));
  const q = world.query(Added(Likes(alice)));
  assert(q.includes(person), 'tracking modifier works across world reset');
  world.reset();
}

console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
EOF

Therefore, I'm confident the intended semantics must be:
- Removed matches whenever a pair removal event occurred during the window, even if the pair was also added during the window.
- Added matches whenever a pair addition event occurred during the window AND the pair is still present (or at least not removed after). Actually if
added and still present, Added matches. If added then removed, Added doesn't match (cancel for Added).
- The "opposite pair events cancel" likely means the Added state cancels (add+remove → no Added). It might also mean Removed state cancels for a
remove-then-add (if pair removed then re-added, no Removed).

So the rule: track two independent flags/counters:
- `added`: set true on add, set false on remove (i.e., an add is canceled by a later remove).
- `removed`: set true on remove, set false on add (i.e., a remove is canceled by a later add).

This gives:
- add then remove: added=false, removed=true → Added no, Removed yes. (This matches trait-level behavior!)
- remove then add: removed=false, added=true → Removed no, Added yes. (Matches trait-level Added behavior.)
- add then destroy(remove): added=false, removed=true → Removed yes. ✓ destruction works.
- add, remove, add: added=true, removed=false → Added yes, Removed no. ✓

This is the correct interpretation. "Opposite pair events on the same target cancel" = each event cancels the opposite tracking state (add cancels a
prior removed; remove cancels a prior added). Symmetric in that sense, but not "net zero" — rather each event toggles the state.

So I should change PairTrackerState from `delta` counter to two booleans `added` and `removed`, or keep counters:
- `added` count: +1 on add, reset/set false on remove? Actually with a counter: increment on add, and on remove set added=false and removed=true. With
booleans it's simplest:
  - on add: `added = true; removed = false;`
  - on remove: `added = false; removed = true;`
  - on change: `changed = true;`

This is the "last event wins per pair" semantics. Let me use booleans.

But wait — what about the global log for late-created queries? With booleans, global state records last event. If add then remove: added=false,
removed=true → new `Removed` query matches. If remove then add: removed=false, added=true → new `Added` matches. That's consistent with trait-level
and with the incremental behavior.

Let me update the type:
```ts
export type PairTrackerState = {
    added: boolean;
    removed: boolean;
    changed: boolean;
    data?: unknown;
};
```

`updatePairTracker`:
- add: state.added = true; state.removed = false;
- remove: state.removed = true; state.added = false;
- change: state.changed = true;

`pairStateMatches`:
- add: state.added
- remove: state.removed
- change: state.changed

`seedPairTrackersFromGlobal`:
- if state.added → update 'add'
- if state.removed → update 'remove'
- if state.changed → update 'change' (and if both added/removed false, only change)

Note: if state.added true and state.changed true, we should seed both add and change. The current seed function with updatePairTracker calls would set
both flags. Let me handle: if added, call update add; if removed, call update remove; if changed, call update change. Since add/remove are mutually
exclusive (last wins), no conflict.

export type PairTrackerState = {
    /**
     * Net add/remove count. Positive means an add is pending, negative means a
     * removal is pending, zero means opposite events on the same target canceled.
     */

export type PairTrackerState = {
    /** Whether the pair is pending as added (last event on this target was an add). */
    added: boolean;
    /** Whether the pair is pending as removed (last event on this target was a remove). */
    removed: boolean;
    /** Whether a change event fired for this pair. */
    changed: boolean;
    /** Last known per-target data (used by query result iteration). */
    data?: unknown;
};"""

    // pending removal and a remove clears a pending addition.
    if (eventType === 'add') {
        state.added = true;
        state.removed = false;
    } else if (eventType === 'remove') {
        state.removed = true;
        state.added = false;
    } else if (eventType === 'change') {
        state.changed = true;

The global events look right: target 1 (alice) has removed=true. The query's group.pairTrackers also has target 1 removed=true. But query entities is
0. So the population loop's `matches` computation must be failing. Let me check: the population loop computes traitMatches for the group. For Removed,
group type 'remove', bitmask for Likes trait. It checks:
- oldMask vs currentMask. Entity still has Likes (bob remains), so currentMask has Likes bit. oldMask (snapshot at factory creation) — snapshot was
taken BEFORE entity spawned, so oldMask for eid=3 is 0. currentMask has Likes bit → `(oldMask & bit) === bit` false; `(oldMask & bit) === 0` true but
`(currentMask & bit) === 0` false → traitMatches false. So the trait part of the AND group fails!

This is the core issue I anticipated: for pair groups, the trait-level bitmask semantics don't apply to non-last pair removals because the entity
still has the trait. The group's trait tracker (which would be set by the incremental event) is not set at population time because the event happened
before query creation and the trait wasn't removed at trait level.

In `checkQueryTracking` incremental path, when the pair remove event fires, it updates the trait tracker (OR bit) AND the pair tracker, so
traitMatched becomes true and match passes. But at population time, the trait tracker is empty; we only seeded pair trackers. The trait part of AND
fails.

So for pair groups, the trait part should be considered satisfied based on pair tracking, not trait tracker, at population time. More generally, for a
group with pairs, the trait-level bitmask tracking should not be required for the base traits that are pair-backed. The group satisfaction should
treat pair-backed traits as satisfied iff their pair state matches.

In the incremental `checkQueryTracking`, when a pair event fires for a matching pair, it sets the trait tracker bit, so traitMatched passes. But for a
non-first pair add/remove, the trait tracker gets set by the pair event. However, consider `Added(Likes(alice), Position)` where Likes(alice) was
added (pair event) and Position was added (trait event). The trait tracker for Likes gets set by the pair event; Position by trait event. Both set →
match. So incremental works.

But for population, we don't have trait events in trackers. We need to compute traitMatched for pair groups differently: the trait part should require
trait events for traits that are NOT pair-backed, and for pair-backed traits require pair state match (not trait tracker).

Actually, simplest: for a group with pairs, in the AND satisfaction, ignore the trait tracker for pair-backed traits entirely and rely solely on pair
trackers for those. For OR, similarly.

Let me modify `checkQueryTracking` and population loop: when a group has pairs, the `traitMatched` computation should only consider traits that are
NOT represented by the group's pairs (i.e., regular traits in the group). The base traits of the pairs are covered by pair state.

How to know which bits in group.bitmasks are pair-backed? For each pair in group.pairs, get its base trait instance generationId/bitflag. Build a set
of `(genId, bitflag)` to exclude from trait tracking requirement.

Let me create a helper `getGroupPairBits(group, ctx)` returning a map genId -> bitmask of pair-backed traits. Then in traitMatched:
- For each gen/mask, `traitMask = mask & ~pairBits[genId]` — only require tracking for non-pair-backed bits. If `traitMask === 0`, no trait
requirement for that gen.
- For OR, any non-pair-backed trait tracker bit OR any pair match.

But wait: in the incremental path, when a pair event fires, we currently set the trait tracker bit for the base trait. If we ignore pair-backed bits
in traitMatched, that's fine (no harm). And for a pair group with only pair traits (no regular traits), traitMatched becomes trivially true for AND,
and for OR traitMatched false (no non-pair bits) but pairMatched handles it.

Let me implement this. I'll add a helper in pair-tracking.ts:

```ts
export function getGroupPairTraitBitmasks(group, ctx): (number | undefined)[] {
    const result: (number | undefined)[] = [];
    for (const pair of group.pairs) {
        const pairTrait = pair[$internal].relation[$internal].trait;
        const inst = getTraitInstance(ctx.traitInstances, pairTrait);
        if (!inst) continue;
        result[inst.generationId] = (result[inst.generationId] || 0) | inst.bitflag;
    }
    return result;
}
```

Then in checkQueryTracking satisfaction:
```ts

// OR: check non-pair trait bits

for genId:
    const mask = groupBitmasks[genId];
    if (!mask) continue;
    const effectiveMask = pairBits ? mask & ~(pairBits[genId] || 0) : mask;
    if (!effectiveMask) continue;
    ...
// AND: require all effective masks
```

And in population loop similarly.

Also, when updating trait trackers in checkQueryTracking for a pair event, we could skip setting pair-backed bits to avoid confusion, but it's
harmless. Actually there's a subtle issue: if we skip updating trait tracker for pair-backed bits, then for `Added(Likes(alice), Position)`, when
Likes(alice) is first added, `addTraitToEntity` fires trait event (target undefined) → in checkQueryTracking, for group with pairs, target undefined →

But cross-event invalidation for pair groups: currently for a pair event matching the group, we apply cross-event invalidation based on groupType vs

For trait-level events (target undefined) on a group with pairs: e.g., `Added(Likes(alice), Position)` — a remove of Position (trait event) should

MESSAGE
The issue is that for pair groups, trait-level bitmask satisfaction should only apply to non-pair-backed traits. Let me add a helper and use it:
CALL s86_c0 TOOL_ID call_00_YFVbSWfjUjDpVU4xJPbn8477
FUNCTION bash
ARGUMENTS
command:
cd /app && python3 - <<'PY'
p='packages/core/src/query/utils/pair-tracking.ts'
s=open(p).read()
# add helper after imports (before updatePairTracker)
helper = """
/**
 * Build a per-generation bitmask of traits that are backed by a relation pair in
 * a tracking group. Those bits are satisfied by pair tracking and should not be
 * required through the trait-level tracker.
 */
export function getGroupPairTraitBitmasks(
    group: { pairs: RelationPair[] },
    world: World
): (number | undefined)[] {
    const result: (number | undefined)[] = [];
    const ctx = world[$internal];
    for (const pair of group.pairs) {
        const pairTrait = pair[$internal].relation[$internal].trait;
        const instance = getTraitInstance(ctx.traitInstances, pairTrait);
        if (!instance) continue;
        result[instance.generationId] = (result[instance.generationId] || 0) | instance.bitflag;
    }
    return result;
}

"""
s=s.replace("/**\n * Update (or create) the per-target pair tracker state for a pair event.\n */", helper + "/**\n * Update (or create) the per-target pair tracker state for a pair event.\n */")
open(p,'w').write(s)
print("helper added")

REASONING_CONTENT
Typecheck passes now. Let me run tests again to make sure nothing broke, then think about edge cases and docs.

But first, let me consider some remaining edge cases and requirements:

1. **Or with pair modifiers and regular traits**: `Or(Added(Likes(alice)), Position)` — Or accepts Trait | Modifier. `Position` is a trait pushed into
Or's traits; `Added(Likes(alice))` is a modifier. In createQueryInstance, Or branch: pushes Position to query.traitInstances.or, processes nested
tracking modifier with logic 'or'. The Or modifier's `traits` (Position) are used as OR trait constraints (entity must have Position OR Added...). The
`checkQueryTracking` static constraints check `or !== 0 && (entityMask & or) === 0 → return false`. So entity must have Position at event time OR...
actually static OR constraint: at least one of the Or traits must be present. For `Or(Added(Likes(alice)), Position)`, the static check requires
entity has Position OR... but Added(Likes(alice)) isn't a static trait; only Position is in the Or static mask. So entity must have Position to pass
static check, which would make it AND-like. That's a pre-existing limitation of Or with tracking modifiers and regular traits — not part of this task.
The task says "Pair modifiers compose with Or" which works (nested modifiers). I won't change Or static semantics.

2. **`Added(Likes(alice), Likes(bob))` (multiple pairs in one modifier)**: The factory creates traits=[Likes,Likes], pairs=[Likes(alice), Likes(bob)].
processTrackingModifier: for each trait, adds Likes bit twice (OR, fine), and pairs dedupe: both distinct pairs added. Group AND: requires
pairStateMatches for both Likes(alice) AND Likes(bob). So entity must have added both. Good.

3. **Query result for multiple pairs in one modifier**: getQueryStores pushes two resolver entries (alice, bob). readEach gives `[aliceData,
bobData]`. Good.

4. **`Changed(Likes('*'))` wildcard**: change event for any target matches. Good.

5. **`Added(Likes('*'))` with `readEach`**: wildcard pair pushes raw base trait (no resolver). readEach gives `{amount: [..]}` (array). Acceptable.

6. **`Or` with two pair modifiers and `hasPairTracking`**: In query.ts, when processing Or's nested modifiers, `processTrackingModifier(..., 'or',
...)` is called. This sets query.hasPairTracking if pairs. `notifyPairEvent` skips queries without hasPairTracking — so a query
`Or(Added(Likes(alice)), Added(Fears(ghost)))` has hasPairTracking true. Pair events reach it. Good. My probe passed.

7. **Trait-only queries and pair events**: `notifyPairEvent` skips queries without hasPairTracking. So pair events don't affect `Added(Likes)`
trait-level query. Good — non-first pair additions don't trigger trait-level Added, and non-last removals don't trigger trait-level Removed. But wait
— for FIRST pair add, `addTraitToEntity` fires trait-level add event for `Added(Likes)` (target undefined), so trait-level Added matches. For LAST
pair remove, `removeTraitFromEntity` fires trait-level remove → trait-level Removed matches. Good, matches existing behavior.

8. **`destroyEntity` and pair events**: When entity destroyed, `removeTrait` for relation trait fires notifyPairEvent for each target, then
`removeTraitFromEntity` fires trait-level remove. Both happen before entity freed. The `notifyPairEvent` iterates `instance.trackingQueries` —
includes pair queries. Good.

But there's a subtle ordering issue: In `destroyEntity`, when the entity is destroyed and has a relation pointing to a target that is also queued for
destruction (autoDestroy target), the target destruction later fires more remove events. Fine.

9. **Global pair events after entity destroyed**: `logPairEvent` stores by eid (entity id). After destruction, eid may be reused for a new entity
(generation changes but eid same). Global events for old entity persist and could pollute new entity with same eid. This is a potential bug, but the
same exists for trait-level dirty masks (dirtyMasks keyed by eid persist). The existing system seems to accept this. Could clear on destroy, but not
required. Let me not worry.

10. **`world.reset()` re-inits tracking masks**: I added the loop. But there's a subtlety: `reset()` clears `ctx.pairTrackingEvents`, then calls
`setTrackingMasks` for each id, which creates fresh empty maps. Good. But the order: I placed the loop AFTER `ctx.worldEntity = createEntity(world,
IsExcluded)`. Let me check the current world.ts reset to make sure `setTrackingMasks` is called before `createEntity`? Let me view.

REASONING_CONTENT
This is fine. `setTrackingMasks` snapshots entityMasks (currently `[[]]`, all zeros) before creating world entity. Then createEntity registers
IsExcluded. That's the same as `world.init` behavior (init calls setTrackingMasks for all ids before registering IsExcluded). Good.

11. **`createTrackingId` cursor and `setTrackingMasks` for all worlds**: When a new factory is created, it loops universe.worlds and calls
setTrackingMasks for each. Good. After world.reset, masks are recreated for all ids up to cursor. Good.

12. **`universe.reset()`**: clears worlds and cachedQueries. Factories remain but their tracking ids are still valid; when a new world is created,
world.init creates masks for all ids up to cursor. Good. Let me test this.

13. **`setTrackingMasks` on a world that was destroyed/recreated**: world.destroy sets universe.worlds[id]=null. A new createWorld may reuse id; its
init loops cursor and calls setTrackingMasks. Good.

14. **The `query-result` change**: `createSnapshotsWithAtomic` now does `{ ...value }` for any object value (not just AoS). For pair-resolved data
that's an object, atomic snapshot is a shallow copy — good for change detection. For arrays (raw base store), `{ ...value }` on an array gives an
object with numeric keys, which is wrong! Previously it only did `{ ...value }` for `ctx.type === 'aos'`. For SoA raw store, value is `{ amount: [..]
}` (object), so `{ ...value }` is fine. For AoS, value is object. But what if raw value is an array? For a relation store, `ctx.get` returns object
with array fields, not an array itself. For regular AoS trait, value is object. So `value && typeof value === 'object' ? { ...value } : value` is okay
for objects; arrays would become object copies but that's only if a trait value is an array (rare). Actually `createSnapshotsWithAtomic` is also used
for regular AoS traits where value could be a class instance or array? Let me check original: `atomicSnapshots[j] = ctx.type === 'aos' ? { ...value }
: null;` — for SoA, atomicSnapshots was null, and change detection used `fastSetWithChangeDetection` which compares scalars. For pair-resolved
entries, we need atomic snapshot to compare object. My change to `{ ...value }` for any object affects SoA raw traits: now atomicSnapshots[j] is `{
amount: [] }` instead of null. In the tracked commit for non-resolver SoA, it uses `fastSetWithChangeDetection` and ignores atomicSnapshots (only AoS
path uses shallowEqual). So no behavior change for non-resolver. Good.

15. **`getPairTrackedData` for Added pair when data is `{}` (empty object)**: `getRelationData` returns `{}` (not undefined) for a relation with no
store? For tag relations, `getRelationData` returns `{}` maybe. Then fallback not needed. Fine.

cd /app/packages/core && cat > tests/pair-tracking-modifiers.test.ts <<'EOF'
import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import {
    $internal,
    createAdded,
    createChanged,
    createRemoved,
    createWorld,
    Or,
    relation,
    trait,
    universe,
} from '../src';

describe('Pair tracking modifiers', () => {
    const world = createWorld();
    world.init();

    beforeEach(() => {
        world.reset();
    });

    it('detects non-first pair additions at pair level', () => {
        const Likes = relation();
        const Added = createAdded();

        const alice = world.spawn();
        const bob = world.spawn();
        const person = world.spawn();

        person.add(Likes(bob));
        person.add(Likes(alice)); // non-first addition

        const q = world.query(Added(Likes(alice)));
        expect(q).toHaveLength(1);
        expect(q).toContain(person);

        // Trait-level Added does not fire for non-first additions.
        const traitLevel = world.query(Added(Likes));
        expect(traitLevel).toHaveLength(0);
    });

    it('detects non-last pair removals at pair level', () => {
        const Likes = relation();
        const Removed = createRemoved();

        const alice = world.spawn();
        const bob = world.spawn();
        const person = world.spawn(Likes(alice), Likes(bob));

        person.remove(Likes(alice)); // non-last removal

        const q = world.query(Removed(Likes(alice)));
        expect(q).toHaveLength(1);
        expect(q).toContain(person);

        // Trait-level Removed does not fire for non-last removals.
        const traitLevel = world.query(Removed(Likes));
        expect(traitLevel).toHaveLength(0);
    });

    it('wildcard target matches any pair', () => {
        const Likes = relation();
        const Removed = createRemoved();

        const apple = world.spawn();
        const banana = world.spawn();
        const person = world.spawn(Likes(apple), Likes(banana));

        person.remove(Likes(apple));

        const q = world.query(Removed(Likes('*')));
        expect(q).toHaveLength(1);
        expect(q).toContain(person);
    });

    it('exclusive replacement produces both a removal and an addition', () => {
        const Targeting = relation({ exclusive: true });
        const Added = createAdded();
        const Removed = createRemoved();

        const a = world.spawn();
        const b = world.spawn();
        const enemy = world.spawn(Targeting(a));

        // Drain initial pair events.
        world.query(Added(Targeting('*')));
        world.query(Removed(Targeting('*')));

        enemy.add(Targeting(b));

        const added = world.query(Added(Targeting(b)));
        const removed = world.query(Removed(Targeting(a)));
        expect(added).toContain(enemy);
        expect(removed).toContain(enemy);
    });

    it('opposite pair events on the same target cancel Added', () => {
        const Likes = relation();
        const Added = createAdded();

        const alice = world.spawn();
        const person = world.spawn();

        person.add(Likes(alice));
        person.remove(Likes(alice));

        expect(world.query(Added(Likes(alice)))).toHaveLength(0);
        // A removal event did occur, so Removed still reports it.
        expect(world.query(Removed(Likes(alice)))).toHaveLength(1);
    });

    it('fires pair-level removal when an entity is destroyed', () => {
        const Likes = relation();
        const Removed = createRemoved();

        const alice = world.spawn();
        const person = world.spawn(Likes(alice), Likes('*'));

        person.destroy();

        const q = world.query(Removed(Likes(alice)));
        expect(q).toHaveLength(1);
        expect(q).toContain(person);
    });

    it('composes with Or', () => {
        const Likes = relation();
        const Fears = relation();
        const Added = createAdded();

        const apple = world.spawn();
        const ghost = world.spawn();
        const person = world.spawn();

        person.add(Likes(apple));

        const q = world.query(Or(Added(Likes(apple)), Added(Fears(ghost))));
        expect(q).toHaveLength(1);
        expect(q).toContain(person);

        // Drain then add the other pair.
        world.query(Or(Added(Likes(apple)), Added(Fears(ghost))));
        person.add(Fears(ghost));
        const q2 = world.query(Or(Added(Likes(apple)), Added(Fears(ghost))));
        expect(q2).toContain(person);
    });

    it('different pair targets produce distinct cached queries', () => {
        const Likes = relation();
        const Added = createAdded();

        const alice = world.spawn();
        const bob = world.spawn();

        const qa = world.query(Added(Likes(alice)));
        const qb = world.query(Added(Likes(bob)));

        expect(qa).not.toBe(qb);

        const ctx = world[$internal];
        // Distinct hashes produce distinct query instances.
        const hashes = [...ctx.queriesHashMap.keys()];
        expect(hashes).toContain((world.query(Added(Likes(alice))) as any).hash);
        expect(hashes).toContain((world.query(Added(Likes(bob))) as any).hash);
    });

    it('combines pair modifiers with regular trait parameters', () => {
        const Likes = relation();
        const Position = trait({ x: 0 });
        const Added = createAdded();

        const alice = world.spawn();
        const withPosition = world.spawn(Position);
        const withoutPosition = world.spawn();

        withPosition.add(Likes(alice));
        withoutPosition.add(Likes(alice));

        const q = world.query(Added(Likes(alice)), Position);
        expect(q).toHaveLength(1);
        expect(q).toContain(withPosition);
        expect(q).not.toContain(withoutPosition);
    });

    it('entity.changed accepts a RelationPair for manual pair-level change signaling', () => {
        const Likes = relation({ store: { amount: 0 } });
        const Changed = createChanged();

        const alice = world.spawn();
        const bob = world.spawn();
        const person = world.spawn(Likes(alice, { amount: 1 }), Likes(bob, { amount: 2 }));

        // Drain.
        world.query(Changed(Likes(alice)));
        world.query(Changed(Likes(bob)));

        person.changed(Likes(alice));

        const changedAlice = world.query(Changed(Likes(alice)));
        const changedBob = world.query(Changed(Likes(bob)));
        expect(changedAlice).toContain(person);
        expect(changedBob).toHaveLength(0);
    });

    it('query result iteration resolves per-target relation data', () => {
        const Contains = relation({ store: { amount: 0 } });
        const Added = createAdded();
        const Removed = createRemoved();

        const gold = world.spawn();
        const inventory = world.spawn();

        inventory.add(Contains(gold, { amount: 42 }));

        let addedData: any;
        world.query(Added(Contains(gold))).readEach(([contains]) => {
            addedData = contains;
        });
        expect(addedData).toEqual({ amount: 42 });

        // Drain and remove.
        world.query(Added(Contains(gold)));
        inventory.remove(Contains(gold));

        let removedData: any;
        const removedQuery = world.query(Removed(Contains(gold)));
        expect(removedQuery).toHaveLength(1);
        removedQuery.readEach(([contains]) => {
            removedData = contains;
        });
        expect(removedData).toEqual({ amount: 42 });
    });

    it('tracking modifiers are long-lived and reused across world resets', () => {
        const Likes = relation();
        const Added = createAdded();

        const alice = world.spawn();
        world.reset();

        const person = world.spawn();
        person.add(Likes(alice));

        const q = world.query(Added(Likes(alice)));
        expect(q).toHaveLength(1);
        expect(q).toContain(person);
    });

    it('tracks Changed on a relation pair specifically', () => {
        const Likes = relation({ store: { amount: 0 } });
        const Changed = createChanged();

        const alice = world.spawn();
        const bob = world.spawn();
        const person = world.spawn(Likes(alice, { amount: 1 }), Likes(bob, { amount: 2 }));

        world.query(Changed(Likes(alice)));
        world.query(Changed(Likes(bob)));

        person.set(Likes(alice), { amount: 10 });

        const changedAlice = world.query(Changed(Likes(alice)));
        const changedBob = world.query(Changed(Likes(bob)));
        expect(changedAlice).toContain(person);
        expect(changedBob).toHaveLength(0);
    });
});

REASONING_CONTENT
Several test issues (some are test bugs, some reveal real problems):

1. Test `detects non-first pair additions` fails at `traitLevel = world.query(Added(Likes))` expecting 0. Actually the first pair add (Likes(bob)) IS
a first pair addition → trait-level Added(Likes) SHOULD match person! So my test expectation is wrong. The trait-level `Added(Likes)` should match
because bob was the first pair added. Let me fix the test: I should check trait-level Added doesn't match for the non-first addition specifically.
Since the query drains, I can drain `Added(Likes)` first after bob add, then add alice, then check trait-level Added(Likes) is 0. Let me restructure:
```
person.add(Likes(bob));
world.query(Added(Likes)); // drain - person matched for first add
world.query(Added(Likes(alice))); // drain
person.add(Likes(alice));

import { $internal } from '../../common';
import type { Entity } from '../../entity/types';
import { getEntityId } from '../../entity/utils/pack-entity';
import type { World } from '../../world';
import type { QueryInstance } from '../types';

/**
 * Check if an entity matches a non-tracking query.
 * For tracking queries, use checkQueryTracking instead.
 */
export function checkQuery(world: World, query: QueryInstance, entity: Entity): boolean {
    const staticBitmasks = query.staticBitmasks;
    const generations = query.generations;
    const ctx = world[$internal];
    const eid = getEntityId(entity);

    if (query.traitInstances.all.length === 0) return false;

    for (let i = 0; i < generations.length; i++) {
        const generationId = generations[i];
        const bitmask = staticBitmasks[i];
        if (!bitmask) continue;

        const required = bitmask.required;
        const forbidden = bitmask.forbidden;
        const or = bitmask.or;
        const entityMask = ctx.entityMasks[generationId]?.[eid] || 0;

        if (!forbidden && !required && !or) return false;
        if (forbidden && (entityMask & forbidden) !== 0) return false;
        if (required && (entityMask & required) !== required) return false;
        if (or !== 0 && (entityMask & or) === 0) return false;
    }

    return true;
}

STEP 107 SOURCE agent
REASONING_CONTENT
All 150 tests pass and typecheck is clean. Now let me think about more edge cases and verify robustness.

### Edge case: query created BEFORE events with pair modifiers combined with regular trait (incremental static check)

`checkQueryTracking` already checks static constraints at event time. Good.

### Edge case: `Or(Added(Likes(alice)))` with `hasPairTracking`

The Or modifier wraps a single tracking modifier. In query.ts, Or branch iterates `parameter.modifiers` and processes nested tracking modifiers with
logic 'or'. This sets query.hasPairTracking. Good.

### Edge case: `Added(Likes(alice))` where query is created after pair add but pair later removed before query run

At population (query created after add, before removal), the global event has added=true → population seeds → entity added to query. Then remove event
fires → notifyPairEvent → checkQueryTracking for query (existing instance now) → groupAffectedByPairEvent true → cross-invalidation for Added group
(remove invalidates add) → returns false → query.remove(entity). So at query run, entity not included. Good — matches "cancel" semantics.

### Edge case: `Removed(Likes(alice))` query created after add+remove

Global event has removed=true → population seeds → match. Good.

### Edge case: `Removed(Likes(alice))` query created before events, then entity added and removed

Incremental: add event → checkQueryTracking for Removed group → cross-invalidation (add invalidates remove) → return false → query.remove (no-op).
remove event → sets removed=true → match. Good.

### Edge case: `Changed(Likes(alice))` where entity also has Likes(bob), changing bob

Per-target: no match for alice. Tested.

### Edge case: `Added(Likes(alice))` with entity that already had Likes(alice) before factory creation

At factory creation, snapshot has no per-pair state (global pair events empty). Entity already has pair; no add event fires. Query created → global
events empty → no match. That's correct: Added should only match events since factory creation.

### Edge case: `Removed(Likes(alice))` with entity that had pair before factory, removed after

No add event in global log (pair existed at factory; no add event fires). Remove event → removed=true. Query created after remove → global has
removed=true → match. Good.

### Edge case: `Added(Likes(alice))` after world.reset with factory created before reset

Factory id exists; reset re-inits masks and pairTrackingEvents empty. New add event → notifyPairEvent → global log. Query created → match. Tested.

### Edge case: `universe.reset()` then create new world with old factory

Let me test. `universe.reset()` clears worlds/cachedQueries but not tracking cursor. New world init creates masks for all ids up to cursor. Old
factory still works. Let me add a probe.

### Edge case: Entity destruction when entity is TARGET of relation

When target destroyed, `destroyEntity` calls `cleanupRelationTarget` for each source → fires notifyPairEvent remove for source. So
`Removed(Likes(alice))` matches the source when alice is destroyed. That's "Entity destruction fires pair-level removal for all active pairs" — for
the source side too. Let me test.

### Edge case: `removeTrait` with relation (not pair) removes all pairs

`removeTrait(world, entity, Likes)` — traitCtx.relation path fires notifyPairEvent per target then removeAllRelationTargets. Tested indirectly.

### Edge case: `getPairTrackedData` returns stale data for a pair that was added then removed in a DIFFERENT query's window

Since global events persist, a `Removed(Likes(alice))` query created much later could match (removed=true persists) and read data from the removal.
That's consistent with trait-level behavior (dirty masks persist). Acceptable.

### Edge case: memory growth of pairTrackingEvents

It only grows on events; acceptable.

### Potential bug: `notifyPairEvent` iterates `instance.trackingQueries` — but for a query with pair tracking, the base trait instance's
trackingQueries includes it. However, `notifyPairEvent` is called from `addRelationPair` BEFORE the query instance may exist (query created later). In
that case, no query to notify; global log handles population. Good.

### Potential bug: In `addRelationPair`, `notifyPairEvent` is called after `addRelationTarget` and data set. But for the FIRST pair,
`addTraitToEntity` already fired trait-level add. `notifyPairEvent` calls `logPairEvent` and then iterates trackingQueries. For a pair query
`Added(Likes(alice))`, checkQueryTracking gets called with target alice. At this point, hasPairTracking true. Good.

But wait — there's a subtle issue: `addTraitToEntity` is called inside `addRelationPair` BEFORE `addRelationTarget`. It fires trait-level add event.
For the pair query `Added(Likes(alice))`, checkQueryTracking with target undefined (trait event) → groupPairs.length>0, target undefined → trait-level
branch → updates trait tracker (Likes bit). Then pair event → updates pair. Both set. Good.

### Potential bug: `processTrackingModifier` adds `modifier.traits` to `query.traitInstances.all`; if a pair modifier has a base trait and the same
trait also appears in a regular required param (e.g., `Added(Likes(alice)), Likes(bob)`), the trait appears in both all and required. Fine.

### Potential bug: `getQueryStores` for `Added(Likes(alice)), Likes(bob)` — params: modifier (pairs [Likes(alice)]) and top-level pair Likes(bob). It
pushes:
- modifier: pair resolver for alice
- top-level pair: pushPairResolver for bob
So readEach gives `[aliceData, bobData]`. Good.

### Potential bug: `getQueryStores` for `Added(Likes(alice), Position)` — modifier.traits = [Likes, Position], pairs=[Likes(alice)]. Push resolver
alice, then skip Likes, push Position raw. Good.

### Potential bug: `Changed(Likes(alice))` readEach data — `getPairTrackedData` tries current data first. Good.

### Potential bug: `entity.changed(Likes(alice))` when pair is gone — `setPairChanged` calls `markChanged` which early-exits if !hasTrait (entity no
longer has Likes). So no event. Fine.

### Potential bug: `checkQueryTracking` uses `groupAffectedByPairEvent` which calls `getTraitInstance` each time. Fine.

### Potential bug: `updatePairTracker` in check-query-tracking.ts doesn't pass data, so per-query pair trackers don't store data; but
getPairTrackedData uses global log which does store data. However, after a query drains, global log still has data. Good.

### Potential issue: `runQuery` clears `query.entities` and calls `resetTrackingBitmasks` which deletes `group.pairTrackers.get(eid)` for returned
entities. But for entities in the result, pair trackers are deleted. If a query result entity is processed and then the query runs again, the pair
state is gone. Good — drained.

But what about entities NOT in the result (e.g., removed from query during window)? Their pair trackers persist. If later a new pair event for them
fires, checkQueryTracking updates existing state and may add them. That's fine and mirrors trait trackers.

### Potential bug: `Or` with pair modifiers sharing same tracking id

If `Added = createAdded()` and query `Or(Added(Likes(alice)), Added(Fears(ghost)))`, both nested modifiers have same id → they merge into one OR
group. Group.pairs = [Likes(alice), Fears(ghost)]. Satisfaction OR: any pair matches. Good.

But there's a subtle issue: `processTrackingModifier` adds BOTH Likes and Fears bitflags to the same group.bitmasks. The groupPairBits includes both.
For OR trait matching, effectiveMask excludes both. Good.

### Potential bug: `Or(Added(Likes(alice)), Added(Likes(bob)))` — same relation, two targets, same id → one OR group with two pairs. Any matches.
Good.

### Potential bug: Cross-query `Added(Likes(alice))` and `Added(Likes(bob))` sharing same modifier id — each creates separate query instance
(different hash). Each has its own group.pairTrackers. When a pair event for alice fires, `notifyPairEvent` iterates instance.trackingQueries (both
queries), calls checkQueryTracking for each. For query Likes(bob), groupAffectedByPairEvent false → skip; for Likes(alice), true → update. Good —
per-target reactivity.

### Potential bug: The `groupAffectedByPairEvent` helper has a `void query` statement and `query` param unused. Let me clean that up (remove query
param). It's harmless but let me clean.

Let me also check the `check-query-tracking.ts` for the `groupAffectedByPairEvent` signature — I pass query but don't use it. Let me remove the query
param.

### Potential issue: `getGroupPairTraitBitmasks` uses `pair[$internal].relation[$internal].trait.id` (public id). The trait instance lookup uses
`getTraitInstance(ctx.traitInstances, pairTrait)`. Good.

import { createWorld, relation, createRemoved, createAdded, universe } from './src';

let pass=0, fail=0;
const assert=(c:any,m:string)=>{ if(c)pass++; else {fail++; console.log('FAIL',m);} };

// universe.reset + old factory in new world
{
  universe.reset();
  const Likes = relation();
  const Added = createAdded();
  const world = createWorld();
  world.init();
  const alice = world.spawn();
  const person = world.spawn();
  person.add(Likes(alice));
  const q = world.query(Added(Likes(alice)));
  assert(q.includes(person), 'factory works after universe.reset + new world');
  world.reset();
}

// target destruction fires pair removal on source
{
  const Likes = relation();
  const Removed = createRemoved();
  const alice = world.spawn();
  const person = world.spawn(Likes(alice));
  world.query(Removed(Likes(alice))); // drain
  alice.destroy();
  const q = world.query(Removed(Likes(alice)));
  assert(q.includes(person), 'target destruction fires pair removal on source');
  world.reset();
}

// Added query created before add, then add + remove cancels Added
{
  const Likes = relation();
  const Added = createAdded();
  const alice = world.spawn();
  const person = world.spawn();
  world.query(Added(Likes(alice))); // create + drain
  person.add(Likes(alice));
  person.remove(Likes(alice));
  const q = world.query(Added(Likes(alice)));
  assert(q.length === 0, 'incremental add+remove cancels Added');
  world.reset();
}

// Removed query created before events, add then remove -> Removed matches
{
  const Likes = relation();
  const Removed = createRemoved();
  const alice = world.spawn();
  const person = world.spawn();
  world.query(Removed(Likes(alice))); // create + drain
  person.add(Likes(alice));
  person.remove(Likes(alice));
  const q = world.query(Removed(Likes(alice)));
  assert(q.includes(person), 'incremental add+remove records Removed');
  world.reset();
}

console.log(`${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
EOF

change events? The existing behavior for untracked traits in updateEach: `ctx.fastSet(eid, store, state[index])` — no change event triggered (that's
the point of "untracked"). So if the user uses `Added(Contains(gold)).updateEach` and modifies data, no change event fires by design (unless the trait
is tracked via onChange or has Changed modifier in the query).

 import { $internal } from '../../common';
 import { Entity } from '../../entity/types';
 import { getEntityId } from '../../entity/utils/pack-entity';
+import { getTraitInstance } from '../../trait/trait-instance';
 import { World } from '../../world';
 import { EventType, QueryInstance } from '../types';
+import { getGroupPairTraitBitmasks, pairStateMatches, updatePairTracker } from './pair-tracking';
+
+/**
+ * Check if a group's relation-pair filters are affected by a pair event.
+ * A wildcard pair target matches any target for the same relation trait.
+ */
+function groupAffectedByPairEvent(
+    group: QueryInstance['trackingGroups'][number],
+    world: World,
+    eventGenerationId: number,
+    eventBitflag: number,
+    target: Entity
+): boolean {
+    if (group.pairs.length === 0) return false;
+    const ctx = world[$internal];
+    const targetId = getEntityId(target);
+    const pairs = group.pairs;
+    const len = pairs.length;
+    for (let i = 0; i < len; i++) {
+        const pair = pairs[i];
+        const pairCtx = pair[$internal];
+        const pairTrait = pairCtx.relation[$internal].trait;
+        const instance = getTraitInstance(ctx.traitInstances, pairTrait);
+        if (!instance) continue;
+        if (instance.generationId === eventGenerationId && (instance.bitflag & eventBitflag)) {
+            const pairTarget = pairCtx.target;
+            if (pairTarget === '*' || pairTarget === targetId) return true;
+        }
+    }
+    return false;
+}

The diff looks good. Now let me think about a subtle issue in `checkQueryTracking`: the pair tracker update block uses `groupAffectedByPairEvent`
which re-checks `getTraitInstance` for each pair. That's fine.

But there's an important subtlety: in the `shouldProcess` logic, for pair groups when target is defined and matches, we update the trait tracker. But
for pair groups with `Added(Likes(alice))`, the trait tracker bit for Likes gets set by the pair event. In satisfaction, `effectiveMask` excludes
Likes, so traitMatched doesn't depend on it. That's fine.

Now another subtle issue: **cross-event invalidation for pair groups on a non-matching target**. I handled it by `shouldProcess=false`. But what about
the pair tracker update block? It also checks `groupAffectedByPairEvent`, so non-matching targets don't update. Good.

Now, let me consider the case where a query has BOTH a pair group and a trait-only group (e.g., `Added(Likes(alice)), Removed(Position)`). When a pair
event for Likes(alice) fires, `notifyPairEvent` iterates instance.trackingQueries (this query has hasPairTracking true). checkQueryTracking:
- For Added(Likes(alice)) group: affectsTrait true, pairMatches true → process, update.
- For Removed(Position) group: affectsTrait false (eventBitflag is Likes, group bitmask is Position) → skip.
- Satisfaction: OR groups? none; AND groups: Added group matches, Removed group traitMatched requires Position tracker — not set → returns false →
query.remove. So entity won't be added to this query on Likes add (because Removed(Position) not satisfied). That's correct AND semantics: both
constraints must be met.

Now, when Position is removed (trait event), `removeTraitFromEntity` fires checkQueryTracking (via query.checkTracking with target undefined) for this
query. For Removed(Position) group: affectsTrait true, groupPairs.length 0 → process, update Position tracker. For Added(Likes(alice)) group:
affectsTrait false → skip. Satisfaction: Added group pairMatched? If Likes(alice) was added earlier, pair tracker has added=true → matches. Removed
group traitMatched (Position removed) → matches. Query.add. Good.

Now, a potential issue: **`notifyPairEvent` only iterates queries with `query.hasPairTracking`**. But what about a query that has a pair group AND a
regular trait group, and a regular trait event fires? That's handled by `addTraitToEntity`/`removeTraitFromEntity` which iterate
`instance.trackingQueries` (all queries including hasPairTracking ones). Good.

Now, let me think about **`markChanged` in changed.ts**: it iterates `data.trackingQueries` and calls checkQueryTracking with target for ALL of them.
For a trait-only `Changed(Likes)` query, passing target is fine (group no pairs). For pair query, target filters. But there's a subtle issue:
`markChanged` sets `changedMask` bit globally for ALL tracking ids (including trait-only Changed ids). So trait-level `Changed(Likes)` gets the
changed bit, and population matches. Good.

Now, a subtle issue with **`setPairChanged` logging data**: It uses `trait[$internal].relation` to get the relation. But `markChanged` early-exits if
`!hasTrait`; if it returns data, the entity has the trait, so relation data exists. Good.

instances still exist (cleared after). Fine.

`Added(Likes(alice))` returns Modifier. So `Or(Added(...))` works type-wise. But `Or`'s `ExtractTraitsFromOrParams` only handles Trait (not

---
title: Entity
description: Entity API
nav: 3
---

An entity is a number encoded with a world, generation and ID. Every entity is unique even if they have the same ID since they will have different generations. This makes automatic-recycling possible without reference errors. Because of this, the number of an entity won't give you its ID but will have to instead be decoded with `entity.id()`.

```js
// Add a trait to the entity
entity.add(Position)

// Remove a trait from the entity
entity.remove(Position)

// Checks if the entity has the trait
// Return boolean
const result = entity.has(Position)

// Gets the trait record for an entity
// Return TraitRecord
const position = entity.get(Position)

// Sets the trait and triggers a change event
entity.set(Position, { x: 10, y: 10 })
// Can take a callback with the previous state passed in
entity.set(Position, (prev) => ({
  x: prev + 1,
  y: prev + 1,
}))

// Get the targets for a relation
// Return Entity[]
const targets = entity.targetsFor(Contains)

// Get the first target for a relation
// Return Entity
const target = entity.targetFor(Contains)

// Get the entity ID
// Return number
const id = entity.id()

// Get the entity generation
// Return number
const generation = entity.generation()

// Destroys the entity making its number no longer valid
entity.destroy()
```

For introspection, `unpackEntity` can be used to get all of the encoded values. This can be useful for debugging.

```js
const { entityId, generation, worldId } = unpackEntity(entity)=====SKILL QUERIES=====
# Queries

Complete guide to querying entities in Koota.

## Contents

- [Basic queries](#basic-queries)
- [Query modifiers](#query-modifiers) - Not, Or
- [Tracking modifiers](#tracking-modifiers) - Added, Removed, Changed
- [Caching queries](#caching-queries) - createQuery for performance
- [Change detection](#change-detection) - updateEach options
- [Query + select](#query--select) - Select subset of traits for updates
- [Direct store access](#direct-store-access) - useStores for performance

## Basic queries

Queries fetch entities that share specific traits (archetypes).

```typescript
// Returns QueryResult (Entity[] with extra methods)
const entities = world.query(Position, Velocity)

// Batch update with updateEach
world.query(Position, Velocity).updateEach(([pos, vel]) => {
  pos.x += vel.x
  pos.y += vel.y
})

// Batch read with readEach
world.query(Position).readEach(([pos], entity) => {
  // ...
})

// Get first match only
const player = world.queryFirst(IsPlayer, Position)

// Query all entities (excludes system entities)
const allEntities = world.query()
```

## Query modifiers

Filter queries with logical modifiers.

```typescript
import { Not, Or } from 'koota'

// Has Position but NOT Velocity
world.query(Position, Not(Velocity))

// Has IsPlayer OR IsEnemy
world.query(Or(IsPlayer, IsEnemy))

// Combine modifiers
world.query(Position, Not(Velocity), Or(IsPlayer, IsEnemy))
```

## Tracking modifiers

Track structural and data changes. Each tracking modifier must be created as a unique instance.

```typescript
import { createAdded, createRemoved, createChanged } from 'koota'

// Create unique instances (typically at module scope)
const Added = createAdded()
const Removed = createRemoved()
const Changed = createChanged()
```

**Added** - Entities that added a trait since last query:

```typescript
const newPositions = world.query(Added(Position))

// Track relation additions
const newChildren = world.query(Added(ChildOf))
```

**Removed** - Entities that removed a trait since last query (includes destroyed entities):

```typescript
const stoppedEntities = world.query(Removed(Velocity))

// Track orphaned entities
const orphaned = world.query(Removed(ChildOf))
```

**Changed** - Entities whose trait data changed since last query:

```typescript
const movedEntities = world.query(Changed(Position))

// Track relation data changes
const updatedChildren = world.query(Changed(ChildOf))
```

**Logical AND (default):**

When multiple traits are passed to a tracking modifier, it uses logical AND. Only entities where **all** specified traits match the condition are returned:

```typescript
// Entities where BOTH Position AND Velocity were added
const fullyAdded = world.query(Added(Position, Velocity))

// Entities where BOTH Position AND Velocity were removed
const fullyRemoved = world.query(Removed(Position, Velocity))

// Entities where BOTH Position AND Velocity have changed
const fullyUpdated = world.query(Changed(Position, Velocity))
```

**Logical OR:**

To track entities where **any** of the specified traits match, wrap individual tracking modifiers in `Or()`:

```typescript
import { Or } from 'koota'

// Entities where EITHER Position OR Velocity was added
const eitherAdded = world.query(Or(Added(Position), Added(Velocity)))

// Entities where EITHER Position OR Velocity was removed
const eitherRemoved = world.query(Or(Removed(Position), Removed(Velocity)))

// Entities where EITHER Position OR Velocity has changed
const eitherChanged = world.query(Or(Changed(Position), Changed(Velocity)))
```

**Key points:**

- Create instances at module scope, not inside functions
- Tracking resets after each query execution
- Changed only tracks `set()` calls and `entity.changed()` signals

## Caching queries

Inline queries hash parameters each call. For hot paths, cache with `createQuery`.

```typescript
import { createQuery } from 'koota'

// Define once at module scope
const movementQuery = createQuery(Position, Velocity)

function updateMovement(world: World) {
  // Fast array-based lookup
  world.query(movementQuery).updateEach(([pos, vel]) => {
    pos.x += vel.x
    pos.y += vel.y
  })
}
```

**When to use:**

- Systems called every frame
- Queries in tight loops
- Performance-critical code

**When inline is fine:**

- Event handlers
- Startup/cleanup code
- Infrequent operations

## Change detection

`updateEach` automatically detects changes for traits tracked via `onChange` or `Changed` modifier.

```typescript
// Default: selective detection (only tracked traits)
world.query(Position, Velocity).updateEach(([pos, vel]) => {
  pos.x += vel.x
})

// Never trigger change events (silent updates)
world.query(Position).updateEach(
  ([pos]) => {
    pos.x += 1
  },
  { changeDetection: 'never' }
)

// Always trigger change events for all mutated traits (DEFAULT)
world.query(Position).updateEach(
  ([pos]) => {
    pos.x += 1
  },
  { changeDetection: 'always' }
)
```

**Shallow comparison:**

Change detection uses shallow comparison like React. Objects and arrays only detect changes if replaced:

```typescript
// ❌ Mutation not detected (same array reference)
world.query(Inventory).updateEach(([inv]) => {
