# Add pair tracking section after Changed relation paragraph
old="""## Add, remove and change events"""
new="""## Relation pair tracking

Tracking modifiers accept relation pairs so you can react to changes for a specific target. The `'*'` wildcard matches any target.

```js
import { createAdded, createRemoved, createChanged } from 'koota'

const Added = createAdded()
const Removed = createRemoved()
const Changed = createChanged()

// Track when an entity starts liking a specific target
const newLikes = world.query(Added(Likes(alice)))

// Track when an entity stops liking a specific target
const removedLikes = world.query(Removed(Likes(alice)))

// Wildcard target tracks any pair
const anyNewLikes = world.query(Added(Likes('*')))

// Track data changes for a specific relation pair
const updatedLikes = world.query(Changed(Likes(alice)))
```

Pair-level tracking is per-target:

- Non-first pair additions (when the entity already targets another entity) are detected.
- Non-last pair removals (when the entity still targets another entity) are detected.
- Replacing an exclusive relation produces both a removal of the old pair and an addition of the new pair.
- Entity destruction fires pair-level removals for all active pairs.
- Opposite add/remove events on the same target cancel: an add followed by a remove no longer reports `Added` for that pair.
- Pair modifiers compose with `Or`: `world.query(Or(Added(Likes(alice)), Added(Fears(ghost))))`.
- Pair modifiers combined with regular traits must satisfy all constraints: `world.query(Added(Likes(alice)), Position)` only returns entities that added the pair AND have `Position`.

Query result iteration resolves per-target data for pair-tracked traits:

```js
const Contains = relation({ store: { amount: 0 } })
const Added = createAdded()

inventory.add(Contains(gold, { amount: 42 }))

world.query(Added(Contains(gold))).readEach(([contains]) => {
  console.log(contains.amount) // 42 (per-target, not the raw store array)
})
```

The `entity.changed()` method also accepts a `RelationPair` for manual pair-level change signaling:

```js
entity.changed(Likes(alice))
```

## Add, remove and change events"""
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new)
open(p,'w').write(s)
print("docs/api updated")

- Entity destruction fires pair-level removals for all active pairs.
- Opposite add/remove events on the same target cancel: an add followed by a remove no longer reports `Added` for that pair.
- Pair modifiers compose with `Or`: `world.query(Or(Added(Likes(alice)), Added(Fears(ghost))))`.
- Pair modifiers combined with regular traits must satisfy all constraints: `world.query(Added(Likes(alice)), Position)` only returns entities that added the pair AND have `Position`.

Query result iteration resolves per-target data for pair-tracked traits:

```js
const Contains = relation({ store: { amount: 0 } })
const Added = createAdded()

inventory.add(Contains(gold, { amount: 42 }))

world.query(Added(Contains(gold))).readEach(([contains]) => {
  console.log(contains.amount) // 42 (per-target, not the raw store array)
})
```

The `entity.changed()` method also accepts a `RelationPair` for manual pair-level change signaling:

```js
entity.changed(Likes(alice))
```

## Add, remove and change events"""
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new)
open(p,'w').write(s)
print("docs/api updated")

Tracking modifiers accept relation pairs for per-target reactivity. The `'*'` wildcard matches any target.

```typescript
const Added = createAdded()
const Removed = createRemoved()
const Changed = createChanged()

// Track a specific pair
const newLikes = world.query(Added(Likes(alice)))
const removedLikes = world.query(Removed(Likes(alice)))
const updatedLikes = world.query(Changed(Likes(alice)))

// Wildcard target tracks any pair
const anyNewLikes = world.query(Added(Likes('*')))
```

Pair tracking is per-target:

- Non-first pair additions and non-last pair removals are detected.
- Exclusive replacement produces both a removal and an addition.
- Entity destruction fires pair-level removals for all active pairs.
- Opposite add/remove events on the same target cancel `Added` reporting.
- Pair modifiers compose with `Or` and combine with regular trait constraints.
- Query iteration resolves per-target relation data (`world.query(Added(Contains(gold))).readEach(([data]) => ...)` gives `{ amount: 42 }`).
- `entity.changed(Likes(alice))` manually signals a pair-level change.

**Key points:**

- Create instances at module scope, not inside functions
- Tracking resets after each query execution
- Changed only tracks `set()` calls and `entity.changed()` signals
- Tracking modifier factories are long-lived and keep working across world resets
"""

   177	/**
   178	 * Add a relation pair to an entity.
   179	 */
   180	/* @inline */ function addRelationPair(world: World, entity: Entity, pair: RelationPair) {
   181	    const pairCtx = pair[$internal];
   182	    const relation = pairCtx.relation;
   183	    const target = pairCtx.target;
   184	
   185	    // Only specific targets can be added (not wildcard '*')
   186	    if (typeof target !== 'number') return;
   187	
   188	    const params = pairCtx.params;
   189	    const relationCtx = relation[$internal];
   190	    const relationTrait = relationCtx.trait;
   191	
   192	    // Ignore if entity already relates to this target
   193	    // For example, adding Likes(alice) when this pair is already on the entity.
   194	    if (hasRelationToTarget(world, relation, entity, target)) return;
   195	
   196	    // For exclusive relations, remove the old target first
   197	    if (relationCtx.exclusive) {
   198	        const oldTarget = getFirstRelationTarget(world, relation, entity);
   199	        if (oldTarget !== undefined && oldTarget !== target) {
   200	            const instance = getTraitInstance(world[$internal].traitInstances, relationTrait);
   201	            if (instance) {
   202	                for (const sub of instance.removeSubscriptions) sub(entity, oldTarget);
   203	            }
   204	            const oldData = getRelationData(world, entity, relation, oldTarget);
   205	            removeRelationTarget(world, relation, entity, oldTarget);
   206	            notifyPairEvent(world, entity, relationTrait, oldTarget, 'remove', oldData);
   207	        }
   208	    }
   209	
   210	    let instance = addTraitToEntity(world, entity, relationTrait);
   211	
   212	    const targetIndex = addRelationTarget(world, relation, entity, target);
   213	    if (targetIndex === -1) return; // No-op
   214	
   215	    const schema =
   216	        instance?.schema ?? getTraitInstance(world[$internal].traitInstances, relationTrait)!.schema;
   217	    const defaults = getSchemaDefaults(schema, relationTrait[$internal].type);
   218	
   219	    if (defaults) {
   220	        setRelationDataAtIndex(world, entity, relation, targetIndex, { ...defaults, ...params });
   221	    } else if (params) {
   222	        setRelationDataAtIndex(world, entity, relation, targetIndex, params);
   223	    }
   224	
   225	    // Fire pair-level tracking for this pair (non-first pair additions are only
   226	    // observable at the pair level).
   227	    notifyPairEvent(world, entity, relationTrait, target, 'add', getRelationData(world, entity, relation, target));
   228	
   229	    // Fire add subscription for this pair
   230	    instance = instance ?? getTraitInstance(world[$internal].traitInstances, relationTrait)!;
   231	    for (const sub of instance.addSubscriptions) sub(entity, target);
   232	}

   234	export function removeTrait(world: World, entity: Entity, ...traits: (Trait | RelationPair)[]) {
   235	    for (let i = 0; i < traits.length; i++) {
   236	        const trait = traits[i];
   237	
   238	        if (isRelationPair(trait)) {
   239	            removeRelationPair(world, entity, trait);
   240	            continue;
   241	        }
   242	
   243	        if (!hasTrait(world, entity, trait)) continue;
   244	
   245	        const traitCtx = trait[$internal];
   246	
   247	        if (traitCtx.relation) {
   248	            // Relation trait: emit per-pair removes, then teardown
   249	            const instance = getTraitInstance(world[$internal].traitInstances, trait);
   250	            if (instance) {
   251	                const targets = getRelationTargets(world, traitCtx.relation, entity);
   252	                for (const t of targets) {
   253	                    for (const sub of instance.removeSubscriptions) sub(entity, t);
   254	                    notifyPairEvent(
   255	                        world,
   256	                        entity,
   257	                        trait,
   258	                        t,
   259	                        'remove',
   260	                        getRelationData(world, entity, traitCtx.relation, t)
   261	                    );
   262	                }
   263	            }
   264	            removeAllRelationTargets(world, traitCtx.relation, entity);
   265	        } else {
   266	            // Regular trait: emit generic remove
   267	            const instance = getTraitInstance(world[$internal].traitInstances, trait);
   268	            if (instance) {
   269	                for (const sub of instance.removeSubscriptions) sub(entity);
   270	            }
   271	        }
   272	
   273	        removeTraitFromEntity(world, entity, trait);
   274	    }
   275	}
   276	
   277	/* @inline */ function removeRelationPair(world: World, entity: Entity, pair: RelationPair) {
   278	    const pairCtx = pair[$internal];
   279	    const relation = pairCtx.relation;
   280	    const target = pairCtx.target;
   281	    const relationTrait = relation[$internal].trait;
   282	
   283	    if (!hasTrait(world, entity, relationTrait)) return;
   284	
   285	    const instance = getTraitInstance(world[$internal].traitInstances, relationTrait);
   286	
   287	    if (target === '*') {
   288	        if (instance) {
   289	            const targets = getRelationTargets(world, relation, entity);
   290	            for (const t of targets) {
   291	                for (const sub of instance.removeSubscriptions) sub(entity, t);
   292	                notifyPairEvent(
   293	                    world,
   294	                    entity,
   295	                    relationTrait,
   296	                    t,
   297	                    'remove',
   298	                    getRelationData(world, entity, relation, t)
   299	                );
   300	            }
   301	        }
   302	        removeAllRelationTargets(world, relation, entity);
   303	        removeTraitFromEntity(world, entity, relationTrait);
   304	        return;
   305	    }
   306	
   307	    if (typeof target === 'number') {
   308	        if (instance) {
   309	            for (const sub of instance.removeSubscriptions) sub(entity, target);
   310	        }
   311	
   312	        const data = getRelationData(world, entity, relation, target);
   313	        const { removedIndex, wasLastTarget } = removeRelationTarget(world, relation, entity, target);
   314	        if (removedIndex === -1) return;
   315	
   316	        notifyPairEvent(world, entity, relationTrait, target, 'remove', data);
   317	
   318	        if (wasLastTarget) removeTraitFromEntity(world, entity, relationTrait);
   319	    }
   320	}

   322	/**
   323	 * Remove a relation target and clean up the base trait if it was the last target.
   324	 * This is used by entity destruction to ensure proper cleanup.
   325	 */
   326	export function cleanupRelationTarget(
   327	    world: World,
   328	    relation: Relation<Trait>,
   329	    entity: Entity,
   330	    target: Entity
   331	): void {
   332	    const relationTrait = relation[$internal].trait;
   333	
   334	    const instance = getTraitInstance(world[$internal].traitInstances, relationTrait);
   335	    if (instance) {
   336	        for (const sub of instance.removeSubscriptions) sub(entity, target);
   337	    }
   338	
   339	    const data = getRelationData(world, entity, relation, target);
   340	    const { removedIndex, wasLastTarget } = removeRelationTarget(world, relation, entity, target);
   341	    if (removedIndex === -1) return;
   342	
   343	    notifyPairEvent(world, entity, relationTrait, target, 'remove', data);
   344	
   345	    if (wasLastTarget) removeTraitFromEntity(world, entity, relationTrait);
   346	}

3. ✅ Exclusive replacement produces both removal and addition. → test.
4. ✅ Modifier factories long-lived and reused across world resets. → test.
5. ✅ Within observation window, opposite pair events on same target cancel. → test (Added canceled; Removed records removal event).
6. ✅ Entity destruction fires pair-level removal for all active pairs. → test.
7. ✅ Pair modifiers compose with Or. → test.
8. ✅ Different pair targets produce distinct cached queries. → test.
9. ✅ Pair modifiers combined with regular trait parameters satisfy all constraints. → test.
10. ✅ entity.changed accepts RelationPair. → test.
11. ✅ Query result iteration resolves per-target relation data. → test.

All covered.

Now, let me double-check one subtle thing about requirement 5: "Within an observation window, opposite pair events on the same target cancel." My
implementation: add then remove → Added canceled, Removed NOT canceled (records removal). Is that "cancel"? If the intended meaning was full symmetric
cancel (neither), then my implementation differs. But as reasoned, full symmetric cancel would break "Entity destruction fires pair-level removal" in
the common case. So my interpretation is correct and consistent with the trait-level system. I'll keep it.

Now, let me also make sure the `getPairTrackedData` fallback doesn't break `Removed(Contains(gold))` when the removed data is an object that gets
mutated by user in a later query. The global state stores a reference to the data object (from `getRelationData` which creates a fresh object). If
user mutates it during iteration, global state's `data` reference would reflect mutation. That could affect later reads. But that's acceptable.

import { $internal } from '../../common';
import type { Entity } from '../../entity/types';
import { getEntityId } from '../../entity/utils/pack-entity';
import { getRelationData } from '../../relation/relation';
import type { Relation, RelationPair } from '../../relation/types';
import { getTraitInstance } from '../../trait/trait-instance';
import type { Trait } from '../../trait/types';
import type { World } from '../../world';
import type { EventType, PairTrackerMap, PairTrackerState } from '../types';
import { checkQueryTrackingWithRelations } from './check-query-tracking-with-relations';


/**
 * Build a per-generation bitmask of traits that are backed by a relation pair in
 * a tracking group. Those bits are satisfied by pair tracking and should not be
 * required through the trait-level tracker.
 */
export function getGroupPairTraitBitmasks(
    group: { pairs: RelationPair[] },
    world: World
): (number | undefined)[] {
    const result: (number | undefined)[] = [];
    const ctx = world[$internal];
    for (const pair of group.pairs) {
        const pairTrait = pair[$internal].relation[$internal].trait;
        const instance = getTraitInstance(ctx.traitInstances, pairTrait);
        if (!instance) continue;
        result[instance.generationId] = (result[instance.generationId] || 0) | instance.bitflag;
    }
    return result;
}

/**
 * Update (or create) the per-target pair tracker state for a pair event.
 */
export function updatePairTracker(
    map: PairTrackerMap,
    eid: number,
    relationTraitId: number,
    targetId: number,
    eventType: EventType,
    data?: unknown
): PairTrackerState {
    let relationMap = map.get(eid);
    if (!relationMap) {
        relationMap = new Map();
        map.set(eid, relationMap);
    }
    let targetMap = relationMap.get(relationTraitId);
    if (!targetMap) {
        targetMap = new Map();
        relationMap.set(relationTraitId, targetMap);
    }
    let state = targetMap.get(targetId);
    if (!state) {
        state = { added: false, removed: false, changed: false };
        targetMap.set(targetId, state);
    }

    // Opposite events on the same target cancel each other: an add clears a
    // pending removal and a remove clears a pending addition.
    if (eventType === 'add') {
        state.added = true;
        state.removed = false;
    } else if (eventType === 'remove') {
        state.removed = true;
        state.added = false;
    } else if (eventType === 'change') {
        state.changed = true;
    }

    if (data !== undefined) state.data = data;

    return state;
}

/**
 * Check whether a tracked relation pair has a matching event state for the given event type.
 * A wildcard target ('*') matches any target recorded for that relation trait.
 */
export function pairStateMatches(
    map: PairTrackerMap | undefined,
    eid: number,
    pair: RelationPair,
    eventType: EventType
): boolean {
    if (!map) return false;
    const relationMap = map.get(eid);
    if (!relationMap) return false;

    const pairCtx = pair[$internal];
    const relationTrait = pairCtx.relation[$internal].trait;
    const targetMap = relationMap.get(relationTrait.id);
    if (!targetMap) return false;

    const pairTarget = pairCtx.target;

    if (pairTarget === '*') {
        for (const state of targetMap.values()) {
            if (eventType === 'add' && state.added) return true;
            if (eventType === 'remove' && state.removed) return true;
            if (eventType === 'change' && state.changed) return true;
        }
        return false;
    }

    if (typeof pairTarget !== 'number') return false;
    const state = targetMap.get(pairTarget);
    if (!state) return false;

    if (eventType === 'add' && state.added) return true;
    if (eventType === 'remove' && state.removed) return true;
    if (eventType === 'change' && state.changed) return true;
    return false;
}

/**
 * Copy global pair events (since factory creation) into a query group's pair trackers.
 * Used when populating newly created query instances.
 */
export function seedPairTrackersFromGlobal(
    map: PairTrackerMap,
    globalEvents: PairTrackerMap | undefined,
    eid: number
): void {
    if (!globalEvents) return;
    const relationMap = globalEvents.get(eid);
    if (!relationMap) return;
    for (const [relationTraitId, targetMap] of relationMap) {
        for (const [targetId, state] of targetMap) {
            if (state.added) {
                updatePairTracker(map, eid, relationTraitId, targetId, 'add', state.data);
            }
            if (state.removed) {
                updatePairTracker(map, eid, relationTraitId, targetId, 'remove', state.data);
            }
            if (state.changed) {
                updatePairTracker(map, eid, relationTraitId, targetId, 'change', state.data);
            }
        }
    }
}

/**
 * Log a pair event into the world-level tracking log so newly created query
 * instances can be populated. Also records the per-target data for iteration.
 */
export function logPairEvent(
    world: World,
    relationTrait: Trait,
    entity: Entity,
    target: Entity,
    eventType: EventType,
    data?: unknown
): void {
    const ctx = world[$internal];
    const eid = getEntityId(entity);
    const targetId = getEntityId(target);

    for (const [id, events] of ctx.pairTrackingEvents) {
        void id;
        updatePairTracker(events, eid, relationTrait.id, targetId, eventType, data);
    }
}

/**
 * Notify all tracking queries that include a relation pair event.
 * Only queries with pair tracking are processed so trait-only queries do not
 * observe pair-level add/remove events (which would incorrectly match
 * non-first additions / non-last removals).
 */
export function notifyPairEvent(
    world: World,
    entity: Entity,
    relationTrait: Trait,
    target: Entity,
    eventType: EventType,
    data?: unknown
): void {
    const ctx = world[$internal];
    const instance = getTraitInstance(ctx.traitInstances, relationTrait);
    if (!instance) return;

    const { generationId, bitflag } = instance;

    // Log globally first so late-created queries can be populated.
    logPairEvent(world, relationTrait, entity, target, eventType, data);

    for (const query of instance.trackingQueries) {
        if (!query.hasPairTracking) continue;

        const match =
            query.relationFilters && query.relationFilters.length > 0
                ? checkQueryTrackingWithRelations(
                      world,
                      query,
                      entity,
                      eventType,
                      generationId,
                      bitflag,
                      target
                  )
                : query.checkTracking(world, entity, eventType, generationId, bitflag, target);
        if (match) query.add(entity);
        else query.remove(world, entity);
    }
}

/**
 * Get the data for a relation pair, used by query result iteration. Prefers live
 * data; falls back to the last recorded event data (needed for Removed pairs).
 */
export function getPairTrackedData(
    world: World,
    entity: Entity,
    relation: Relation<Trait>,
    target: Entity
): unknown {
    const data = getRelationData(world, entity, relation, target);
    if (data !== undefined) return data;

    // Fall back to pair event state (global log) for removed pairs.
    const ctx = world[$internal];
    const eid = getEntityId(entity);
    const targetId = getEntityId(target);
    const relationTrait = relation[$internal].trait;

    for (const events of ctx.pairTrackingEvents.values()) {
        const relationMap = events.get(eid);
        if (!relationMap) continue;
        const targetMap = relationMap.get(relationTrait.id);
        if (!targetMap) continue;
        const state = targetMap.get(targetId);
        if (state && state.data !== undefined) return state.data;
    }

    return undefined;
}

MESSAGE
I've completed the implementation. Let me provide a summary:

## Summary

I implemented per-target relation pair tracking for the `Added`, `Removed`, and `Changed` query modifiers in a new branch `pair-tracking-modifiers`.

### Core changes

1. **Tracking modifier factories accept `RelationPair`** — `createAdded`, `createRemoved`, and `createChanged` now accept traits, relations, or
relation pairs. `'*'` targets act as wildcards.

2. **Per-target reactivity** — Pair-level events are fired from all relation add/remove paths:
   - Non-first pair additions (entity already targets another entity)
   - Non-last pair removals (entity still targets another entity)
   - Exclusive replacement → both a removal of the old pair and an addition of the new pair
   - Entity destruction → pair-level removals for all active pairs
   - Target destruction → pair-level removal on the source

3. **Cancellation semantics** — Within an observation window, opposite events on the same target cancel: an add followed by a remove no longer reports
`Added` for that pair (and vice versa), consistent with trait-level semantics.

4. **`Or` composition** — Pair modifiers compose with `Or`, including across different tracking factories.

5. **Distinct cached queries** — Pair targets are encoded in the query hash so `Added(Likes(alice))` and `Added(Likes(bob))` produce distinct cached
query instances.

6. **Combined constraints** — Tracking query population now enforces static trait constraints, so `world.query(Added(Likes(alice)), Position)`
requires both the pair addition and `Position`.

7. **`entity.changed(RelationPair)`** — Supports manual pair-level change signaling.

8. **Per-target query iteration** — `readEach`/`updateEach` resolve per-target relation data (`{ amount: 42 }`) instead of raw store arrays, including
for removed pairs via captured event data.

9. **Long-lived factories** — Tracking modifier masks are re-initialized on `world.reset()` so factories keep working across resets.

### Verification
- Added `packages/core/tests/pair-tracking-modifiers.test.ts` with 13 tests covering all requirements.
- All 150 core tests + 35 react tests pass.
- TypeScript typecheck and oxlint pass.
- Updated docs (`docs/api/query-modifiers.md`, `docs/api/entity.md`) and the koota skill reference.
